People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

I mean, I’m trying not to make too much of it, but that’s about when those thoughts started.\n\nDoctor: Have you been enjoying the things you usually like to do over the past two weeks?\n\nPatient: Not really, no… I mean, I still try to keep up appearances, but it’s not the same. I used to mess with my tomato plants, sand wood out at my bench, watch the Falcons, stay after church and talk with folks. Lately I just kind of look at all that like it belongs to somebody else, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any pleasure when doing things you normally enjoy over the past two weeks?\n\nPatient: A little maybe, but… not like before. I’m not trying to be dramatic, that’s just how it’s been.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling restless or having trouble sitting still lately?\n\nPatient: Restless? No… not really, I don’t think so. If anything it’s kind of the opposite. I’ve been slower, like walking slower at work, and people keep asking if I’m sick. Even answering simple questions takes me longer than it should. But I do keep going back over my daughter leaving, and I keep thinking I must’ve failed her somehow. Like… if I’d been a better father, maybe I would’ve done something different. I know maybe that’s not fair, but it’s hard not to put it on myself.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling like you're fidgeting or unable to sit still when you're trying to relax?\n\nPatient: Not really fidgeting, no. People at work keep asking if I’m sick because I’m walking slower, and even simple questions take me forever to answer. I don’t mean to make it sound worse than it is, but it feels like my body’s moving through mud.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any days in the past two weeks where you felt somewhat better, or has it been consistently difficult the entire time?\n\nPatient: I guess it’s been pretty much the whole time… not every minute is awful, I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but this change in me started about two weeks ago, after my daughter left Georgia for the military. Lately I just kind of look at all that like it belongs to somebody else, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any pleasure when doing things you normally enjoy over the past two weeks?\n\nPatient: A little maybe, but… not like before. I’ll go out to my tomato plants or sit at my bench with the wood, and I’m kind of just there. Even watching the Falcons or talking after church, it feels like I’m looking at somebody else’s life, if that makes sense. I don’t want to overstate it, but the pleasure’s mostly not there lately.\n\nDoctor: Have you thought about how you would carry out those thoughts of not waking up?\n\nPatient: No… not like a plan or anything. It’s more been that thought of, maybe my family would be better off if I just didn’t wake up. I know that sounds bad, and I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but it scared me enough that I told my supervisor and came in.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your energy level or how quickly you move compared to before these thoughts started?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I have. I don’t want to make too much of it, but people at work keep asking me if I’m sick or something. I’ve been walking slower, and when somebody asks me a simple question, it takes me a while to get the answer out. It’s like my body’s moving through mud, if that makes sense. I’m trying to keep up, but I know I’m not moving like I usually do.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in your weight since these thoughts started?\n\nPatient: I haven’t really been checking the scale, so I don’t want to say a number and be wrong. But… yeah, my eating’s been off. Most days I kind of have to make myself take a few bites, and stuff I’d normally like, whether it’s at the warehouse or at home, just sits there until I end up throwing it out. I’m not trying to be dramatic, that’s just how it’s been.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling restless or having trouble sitting still lately?\n\nPatient: Restless? No… not really, I don’t think so. If anything it’s kind of the opposite. I’ve been slower, like walking slower at work, and people keep asking if I’m sick. Even answering simple questions takes me longer than it should. It feels like my body’s moving through mud, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty about anything or like you're worthless lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t want to sound like I’m feeling sorry for myself or anything. But I do keep going back over my daughter leaving, and I keep thinking I must’ve failed her somehow. Like… if I’d been a better father, maybe I would’ve done something different. I know maybe that’s not fair, but it’s hard not to put it on myself.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling like you're fidgeting or unable to sit still when you're trying to relax?\n\nPatient: Not really fidgeting, no. I don’t think I’ve been unable to sit still. It’s more… the other way around, if that makes sense. People at work keep asking if I’m sick because I’m walking slower, and even simple questions take me forever to answer. I don’t mean to make it sound worse than it is, but it feels like my body’s moving through mud.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any days in the past two weeks where you felt somewhat better, or has it been consistently difficult the entire time?\n\nPatient: I guess it’s been pretty much the whole time… not every minute is awful, I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but this change in me started about two weeks ago, after my daughter left Georgia for the military. Since then it’s been consistently hard, I’d say.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling like you're fidgeting when you're alone?\n\nPatient: Not really when I’m alone, no… I wouldn’t say I’m fidgeting. If anything, it’s kind of the opposite. I’ve been moving slower, and people at work have even asked if I’m sick. Sometimes I take forever just to answer a simple question. It feels like my body’s moving through mud, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any thoughts that seemed unreal or heard any voices that others couldn't hear over the past two weeks?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I haven’t heard voices or anything like that. And I don’t think I’ve had thoughts that felt unreal… I mean, I’ve been upset, but not like that, if that’s what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any difficulty concentrating on things like reading or watching TV over the past two weeks?\n\nPatient: Yeah… a little, I guess. I don’t do much reading, but at the distribution center I’ll be looking at the schedule or the routing sheet, and my mind just won’t really lock in. Stuff I’d normally sort out pretty quick, like small staffing calls, starts feeling bigger than it should. I can still get through it, mostly… but it takes me longer than usual, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any days in the past two weeks where you didn't get out of bed or leave the house?\n\nPatient: I mean… I’ve gotten out of bed, yeah. I don’t think I’ve just stayed in the house all day like that. But I haven’t been handling things the way I usually do.\n\nWork’s been… not great. I made enough mistakes that one morning a supervisor found me sitting in my truck before dawn, and they ended up getting occupational health involved. That was kind of embarrassing. And at home, bills and things I normally keep up with have been piling up. I’m not trying to make it sound worse than it is, but… it hasn’t been like me.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts of death, besides thinking your family would be better off without you?\n\nPatient: I… yeah. I mean, not like I’ve made some plan or anything, but the reason I came in is because I kept having this thought that maybe my family would be better off if I just didn’t wake up. And that scared me enough that I told my supervisor. I don’t like saying it, but… that’s the thought that’s been there.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling like you're fidgeting or restless when you're sitting still?\n\nPatient: Not so much fidgety, I don’t think. It’s more the other way, if that’s okay to say.\n\nPeople at work have been asking if I’m sick because I’m walking slower, and I guess I’m taking a long time to answer simple questions. It feels like my body’s moving through mud sometimes. I don’t mean to be dramatic, but… that’s what it feels like.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling like you're fidgeting or restless when you're sitting still, lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I know I mentioned it, but it’s not really like I’m fidgeting or can’t sit still. It’s more that I’m slowed down, I guess.\n\nPeople at work keep asking if I’m sick, because I’m walking slower and I’m taking forever to answer simple questions. It feels kind of like my body’s moving through mud. I don’t know if that’s the kind of thing you mean.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods in the past two weeks where you felt nearly normal, without these thoughts and feelings?\n\nPatient: Not really, no… I mean, I’ve had little moments where I can keep going and act normal, I guess, but this whole change in me has pretty much been there for about two weeks now.\n\nIt started after my daughter left Georgia for the military. Since then I haven’t really felt like myself. Is that what you mean?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing.
